# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-18

This edition provides 76 complete documentation guides and 51 additional screened leads. 27 earlier screened entries received full guides. New and updated records carry their own dated evidence checks. Creative workflows include optical score recognition, camera-tracked shadow figures, editable voice-and-caption handoffs, subject mattes and depth layers, and editable local image masks. A current AutoShade guide covers material changes in the v1.18 line following the earlier v1.15 review. Software licensing is reviewed independently of model terms, any paid service or required proprietary host. NLLB translation and a browser background-removal route use separate noncommercial model grants. No discovered app was installed or run. The frozen plan covered 110 fields with 1222/1222 repository queries attempted. 1182 completed successfully, 8 were partial, and 32 failed. 227 were bounded. All 29 configured model tasks were attempted. 0 model tasks failed. The collection retained explicit warnings and limits. Metadata screening indexed all 2,543 saved repository pages, spanning 81,587 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog was consulted alongside today's metadata, including earlier pilot candidates. It currently has 113,692 raw records and 1,118 previously screened pending guides. A metadata signal does not establish software eligibility. Unreviewed and excluded candidates remain in private research records. Upstream credential-shaped metadata and signed media queries were redacted before sealing, with original hashes and redaction provenance retained locally. Live primary research beyond GitHub covered all observed fields and the configured ecosystems. The notes identify limits such as access and rate restrictions, failed fetches and bounded discovery pages. The counts represent the documented searches and available pages. The first aggregate pilot repository-tree index was reconstructed from the original saved response bodies; exact initial request timestamps for that aggregate were not recovered. Model-task responses with no rows: text2text-generation. Current cited reviews changed 2 prior labels. Vidyom moved from Quality unverified to Experimental. LiveKit Lite Avatar moved from Quality unverified to Experimental. The separate watchlist and evidence enrichment recorded 59 individual watchlist failures and 3 evidence failures. Source failures are retained as gaps.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

110 creative fields; 1222/1222 repository queries attempted; 29/29 model-task queries attempted. 82328 distinct source candidates and 1275 model leads. 76 detailed profiles and 51 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 32 failed repository queries, 8 partial repository queries, 227 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Live repository topics and file APIs were researched. GridFab’s source tree, package configuration and full AGPL grant are available from the canonical GitLab repository. Its PyPI release and pre-alpha status support a scoped editor guide. The first guessed license filename returned 404; the actual LICENSE.md was recovered. OpenSlides returned an access error. These are bounded source checks. [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/creative-coding) [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-editor) [Source](https://gitlab.com/azrazalea/gridfab) [Source](https://gitlab.com/azrazalea/gridfab/-/blob/main/LICENSE.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/openslides/openslides)
- **codeberg** · searched: The default-branch API, repository trees and complete grants yielded current evidence for Imaginer and Raspberry Pi 5 Diffusion. Imaginer’s initial main-branch paths were wrong. The repository specifies master and COPYING. Multiple Codeberg topic search APIs returned 429; current site/file visibility did not clear the incomplete discovery coverage. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer/raw/branch/master/COPYING) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion/raw/branch/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=mediapipe&limit=20)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Checked live project search pages for diffusion, whisper, creative work, captions and OSC, and revalidated the diffuser Cargo manifest and complete license. The local-path dependency is a setup limit for that existing entry. The breadth and installation of other tools were not established from these pages. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=creative) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=whisper) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=caption) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Reviewed live Python, JavaScript and Rust registry metadata, including GridFab’s Python/Tk release and the score-reader and MediaPipe components. Some guessed package names returned 404. Listings show component/version context without verifying complete source licenses or results. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/gridfab/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/score-reader/json) [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mediapipe%2Ftasks-vision/latest) [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/beatnet-rs) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/manim-voiceover/json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Current Interstice and Krita pages give plugin integration context. The SpriteLoom docs separate its original Aseprite route and the MLX or agent adapters. A proprietary creative host, optional model and add-on software keep separate terms and requirements. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://kritaaidiffusion.com/) [Source](https://vkarach.github.io/spriteloom/) [Source](https://github.com/AlexPascu007/spriteloom-mlx) [Source](https://www.reaper.fm/reaplugs/)
- **project-sites** · searched: Live primary research covers every observed field and includes project sites, registries, research abstracts and direct source pages. AutoShade’s current site and releases distinguish reverse fitting from optional image generation. Unavailable pages, JavaScript shells and bounded captures retain their stated gaps. [Source](https://autoshade.dev/) [Source](https://ossia.io/generative-art.html) [Source](https://imaginer.codeberg.page/help/local/) [Source](https://senacula.github.io/senacula-shapestudio/) [Source](https://voiceover.manim.community/en/stable/api.html)
- **research-code** · searched: Duke’s learned robotic shadow article and FoleyForge and ATune papers supply current research context. The examined primary material did not establish reusable code grants for those exact research systems. eShadow provides a distinct released implementation, and its learned segmentation route is narrower than learning a puppeteer’s movements. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22434) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08695) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16236) [Source](https://github.com/kirisaki-momotaro/eShadow-LCE)
- **international** · searched: Reviewed cross-language sources on Mandarin interaction, Greek/Flutter puppetry, Chinese Jianpu score workflows and Vietnamese subtitle localization. Sunway and CUNY projects add a different shadow-performance context. Their documentation does not itself clear artwork and recording terms or prove release availability. [Source](https://sunwayuniversity.edu.my/research/centres/SCDHCH/woyae-tangae) [Source](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/ny_pubs/1252/) [Source](https://github.com/QuentinCrane/ShadowLite) [Source](https://github.com/OctaveMelody/OctoPus) [Source](https://github.com/htcuong27/subtitle-translator)

## ShadowLite

AI shadow puppetry and hand-shadow performance · Neural rigging and digital puppetry · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync

This completes an earlier pending guide in the library. The current source review is dated 2026-11-18.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototype a Chinese-language shadow-theatre character that responds to a typed line or a short spoken scene cue.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-18. No local execution implied.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. README names vosk-model-cn-0.22 while main.py expects vosk-model-small-cn-0.22. Voice setup needs a compatible model placement. Font and puppet assets and sustained live performance remain unverified.

### How it uses AI

Vosk turns Mandarin speech into text, then the local Gemma-driven agents select a conversational reply and matching 2D puppet actions for a digital character. 1. Start the local Ollama service and launch from the working folder. Leave the microphone off and enter a short Chinese line in Text Mode.
2. Observe the reply and action together. Confirm the compatible Vosk model directory before switching to voice and use Space to start and stop a short recording.
3. Inspect the recognized text before trusting the response or action. Preserve the animation and cue separately if used as performance reference.

### How it uses AI

The Flask server uses rembg and a lightweight U-2-Net ONNX model to isolate foreground images for puppets. Unity renders and animates the figures and phones send collaborative control messages. ### How it uses AI

Lilyscan uses Audiveris optical music recognition and Tesseract for score text, repairs common recognition errors and builds editable LilyPond parts for review.
```sh
docker compose up -d
```


```sh
LANG=C.UTF-8 ./gradlew :lilyscan-cli:shadowJar
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/vilpter/lilyscan/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/vilpter/lilyscan/blob/main/docs/INSTALL.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/vilpter/lilyscan/blob/main/docker-compose.yml) [Source 4](https://audiveris.github.io/audiveris/) [Source 5](https://github.com/vilpter/lilyscan/issues/96) [Source 6](https://github.com/vilpter/lilyscan/issues/97)

1. Upload one clean printed page or photo through the local browser interface, ideally for one instrument.
2. Compare notes and rhythm in the source and engraved panes. Edit the marked LilyPond measures, save and recompile.
3. Download LilyPond and MusicXML as needed. Cross-check slurs, repeats, octave marks and transpositions against the source.

- **Platforms:** Docker instructions target x86-64 Linux. Native Linux and Windows are documented. The maintainer marks macOS untested. Browser clients access the configured server.

### How it uses AI

ScoreBrush turns the musician’s markings on a draft transcription into a prompt or file handoff to an external AI that applies LilyPond corrections. The musician then reviews each change. ### How it uses AI

Conventional image processing isolates staves, then the homr transformer recognizes printed notation per staff. The tool merges the results into MusicXML with a review UI. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Isolated glyph classification does not reconstruct staff order, rhythm or score structure. The test shells demonstrate integration surface; independent stylus reliability remains unknown.

### How it uses AI

A neural classifier predicts music glyphs from a cropped image or rasterized stylus strokes. It exports the weights and input specification for embedded notation-editor experiments. [Source](https://github.com/vibetuned/granita/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/vibetuned/granita/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Introduction

A neural classifier predicts music glyphs from a cropped image or rasterized stylus strokes. It exports the weights and input specification for embedded notation-editor experiments. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented uv environment and select a released model and its matching sidecar. The optional export environment installs more conversion tools.

```sh

```


```sh

```

2. Check the candidate classes and test two visually similar symbols before integrating the classifier.
3. Use the sidecar’s normalization, rasterization and optional context rules if embedding in an editor.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The uncompressed GPU container is documented as about 11 GB. Owner notes that TensorRT RIFE on Blackwell falls back to CUDA/PyTorch.

### How it uses AI

The VapourSynth pipeline combines optional descale and deband operations with neural Spandrel upscale and RIFE frame interpolation before H.265 encoding. [Source](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

The VapourSynth pipeline combines optional descale and deband operations with neural Spandrel upscale and RIFE frame interpolation before H.265 encoding. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Compare upscale and motion-interpolation results on a short animation restoration test while retaining the source frames. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer provides benchmark notes and hosted-run recipes. These are owner tests and do not establish a visually preferred result on new source footage. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

Use the platform-specific README or the documented NVIDIA Docker route. Fetch and verify pinned model files and turn on only the stages needed for a short local input. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the platform-specific README or the documented NVIDIA Docker route. Fetch and verify pinned model files and turn on only the stages needed for a short local input.

```sh
docker compose build
```


```sh
docker compose run --rm anime-pipeline models verify
```


```sh
uv run anime-pipeline models verify
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose a short rights-cleared animation sequence with moving lines and an edit point.
2. Compare upscale only with optional interpolation and inspect line wobble, halos and duplicated frames.
3. Keep temporary previews and outputs local while deciding whether to batch the same settings.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/4q4r/anime_upscale/blob/main/README.md)

- **Platforms:** Linux and Windows instructions and Kaggle/Colab recipes are documented. The README notes CUDA fallback for Blackwell interpolation. macOS is not established.

### Hardware & software

[Source 1](https://github.com/senacula/senacula-shapestudio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://senacula.github.io/senacula-shapestudio/) [Source 3](https://senacula.github.io/senacula-shapestudio/mcp.html) [Source 4](https://github.com/senacula/senacula-shapestudio/blob/main/TRADEMARK.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/senacula/senacula-shapestudio/releases/tag/v0.1.7)

- **Hardware:** Owner lists TripoSR at 8 GiB system RAM minimum and 16 recommended. TRELLIS lists 16 GiB NVIDIA VRAM minimum and 24 recommended. Model and runtime downloads can be several GB.
- **Software:** PyTorch and selected checkpoint, with optional CUDA and UI components. Packaged and Python dependencies vary by host.
- **Platforms:** Publisher documents Windows packages, Apple Silicon macOS and x86-64 Linux plus browser and CLI frontends. The best-tier model requires NVIDIA and is not available on CPU.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

### How it uses AI

Uses learned segmentation, pose and depth estimation to create separate video mattes, conditioning depth/pose layers and estimated BVH motion from ordinary clips. Uses learned segmentation, pose and depth estimation to create separate video mattes, conditioning depth/pose layers and estimated BVH motion from ordinary clips. ### How it uses AI

Integrates browser-local neural subject segmentation, masking, inpainting and super resolution into a non-destructive layer editor, with optional separate ComfyUI or LLM workflows. [Source](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

### Introduction

Integrates browser-local neural subject segmentation, masking, inpainting and super resolution into a non-destructive layer editor, with optional separate ComfyUI or LLM workflows. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

### What it is good for

Produce editable foreground cutouts, compare AI edit variants and preserve an original layer for review. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

### Demo & examples

Developer docs describe point-guided SAM selections, inpainting and layer branches for alternate results. No independent edge-detail or WebGPU behavior review was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

### Install

Run the source workspace with Node.js 20+ and pnpm, then open it in a WebGPU-compatible browser. Local modes select and download specific models on first use. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Run the source workspace with Node.js 20+ and pnpm, then open it in a WebGPU-compatible browser. Local modes select and download specific models on first use.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

1. Load an authorized test image into an editable layer.
2. Select a local model that fits the licensing terms, compare mask edges manually and make a new branch.
3. Export a lossless review copy and compare to the source before enabling any remote bridge.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+, pnpm, WebGPU-compatible browser and compatible ONNX model package. Optional separate ComfyUI or LLM providers.
- **Platforms:** The README lists Chrome, Edge and Safari with WebGPU. OS and device support follow that configuration and were not inferred.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/gpex-cloud/opengpex/blob/v2-webgpu/package.json) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/briaai/RMBG-1.4)

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** GPL-3.0 software. The documented BRIA RMBG 1.4 checkpoint has separate noncommercial terms. Other segmentation, inpainting and upscaling models have their own grants.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0 software. The documented BRIA RMBG 1.4 checkpoint has separate noncommercial terms. Other segmentation, inpainting and upscaling models have their own grants.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. ### Limitations

Local built-in inference and a remote bridge use different data paths. Actual performance and maximum canvases depend on GPU, browser, model and image size. Convert an agent rough cut into an editable review, fix the timing by hand and preserve original media. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The author documents multi-track interaction and the agent handoff. End-to-end independent round-tripping and source reconnect are unverified. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the repository’s Electron/Remotion source instructions. Configure the optional MCP bridge only when connecting a compatible agent. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the repository’s Electron/Remotion source instructions. Configure the optional MCP bridge only when connecting a compatible agent.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

1. Have the documented agent path produce a short test assembly.
2. Open it in an independent work copy and check source paths, track order and edit timecodes.
3. Fix cuts, export a review and compare it with the originally proposed sequence.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Electron, Node.js project dependencies, Remotion and FFmpeg. Optional agent access supplied by the creator.
- **Platforms:** Desktop source workflow. Packaged platform support and codec-specific compatibility are unverified.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT source. Remotion, external agent services, FFmpeg distributions and media assets retain separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT source. Remotion, external agent services, FFmpeg distributions and media assets retain separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/RiccardoFalco06/selects-editor/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

Detects and tracks a person’s 2D and root-relative 3D body keypoints from a single RGB camera, with local Python and browser routes. ### Introduction

Detects and tracks a person’s 2D and root-relative 3D body keypoints from a single RGB camera, with local Python and browser routes. The source guide lists package setup and a webcam/CLI route. Browser operation needs a compatible WebGPU/ONNX Runtime Web environment and the separately distributed ONNX artifacts. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The source guide lists package setup and a webcam/CLI route. Browser operation needs a compatible WebGPU/ONNX Runtime Web environment and the separately distributed ONNX artifacts.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/yuru-lab/open-body-tracker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://yuru-lab.github.io/open-body-tracker/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Yurup/open-body-tracker/raw/main/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/Yurup/open-body-tracker/raw/main/LICENSE)

1. Use one consenting performer under controlled camera placement.
2. Inspect 2D joints and 3D pose through a short movement and note occlusion gaps.
3. Check the source-to-avatar coordinate mapping and continuity before reusing motion.
